\mathcal{O}(\|\mathcal{R}\|)
time.
The run time is dominated by the computation of
\mathsf{NF}^{\circ}
,
which takes
\mathcal{O}(\|\mathcal{R}\|^{3})
time.

Example
\thethm

(continued from Examples
3.4
and
3.5
)
.

For
\mathcal{U}
the
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{U}}/\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{U}}
-reducible terms are

\neg\mathsf{NF}^{\circ}_{\mathcal{U}}=\{[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{a}],[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}]\}
.
Note that normal forms also include terms like

[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{f}]
or
[\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}]\circ[\mathsf{a}]

that have no correspondence in the original TRS.
For
\mathcal{V}
we obtain the following
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{V}}/\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{V}}
-reducible terms:

\neg\mathsf{NF}^{\circ}_{\mathcal{V}}=\{[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{b}],[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}],[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}],[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{a}],[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}],[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}]\}
.

In the
\mathcal{U}
case,
we have
\mathsf{TS}([\mathsf{f}]_{\mathcal{U}}\circ[\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}})

(e.g., because
[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{b}]\in\mathsf{NF}_{\mathcal{U}}
)
and
\mathsf{TS}([\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}})
,
whereas for
\mathcal{V}
no top-stabilizable sides or constants exist.

6.3.
Checking
NFP

We base the procedure on
the decision procedure for
UNC
(Listing
8
).
A closer look at the
NFP
-condition reveals
that they are fairly easy to check,
provided one starts with an enumeration of all pairs
(w(q),q)

where
w(q)
is defined.
The
UNC
decision procedure works by doing exactly that:
each item pushed to its
worklist
corresponds to
a pair
(w(q),q)
,
and if
UNC
holds, each such pair is enumerated exactly once.
Therefore we can modify the procedure to check
NFP
instead of
UNC
,
see Listing
14
.

1:

compute
\mathcal{C}
and a representation of
\mathcal{N}

2:

let
\mathit{seen}(p)
be undefined for all
p\in\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}
(to be updated below)

3:

compute
\mathcal{F}
and top-stabilizable sides
\mathsf{TS}

4:

for all
constants
c\trianglelefteq\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
that are normal forms
do

5:

push
([c]_{\mathcal{R}},[c],c)
to
worklist

6:

if
any constant
p\in\Sigma^{[]}
with
(p)_{\mathcal{R}}=[c]_{\mathcal{R}}

does not satisfy
p\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{F}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}}[c]

then

7:

return

\text{NFP}(\mathcal{R})
is false
—
first
NFP
-condition violated

8:

if
there is any
p_{1}\circ p_{2}\in\mathsf{TS}
with

p_{1}\circ p_{2}\to[c]_{\mathcal{R}}\in\mathcal{C}

then

9:

return

\text{NFP}(\mathcal{R})
is false
—
second
NFP
-condition violated

10:

while

worklist
not empty
do

11:

(p,q,s)\leftarrow
pop
worklist

12:

if

\mathit{seen}(p)
is defined
then

13:

return

{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.4688,0.4688}\text{NFP}}(\mathcal{R})
is false
—
not
UNC

14:

\mathit{seen}(p)\leftarrow(q,s)

15:

for all
transitions
p_{1}\circ p_{2}\to p_{r}\in\mathcal{C}
with
p\in\{p_{1},p_{2}\}

do

16:

if

\mathit{seen}(p_{1})=(q_{1},s_{1})
and
\mathit{seen}(p_{2})=(q_{2},s_{2})
are defined
then

17:

if
there is a transition
q_{1}\circ q_{2}\to q_{r}\in\mathcal{N}

then

18:

push
(p_{r},q_{r},s_{1}\circ s_{2})
to
worklist

19:

G\leftarrow\{p^{\prime}\mid p^{\prime}_{1}\circ p^{\prime}_{2}\to p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E},\,p_{1}=(p^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{R}},\,p_{2}=(p^{\prime}_{2})_{\mathcal{R}}\}

20:

if
there is
q\in\Sigma^{[]}
with
(q)_{\mathcal{R}}=p_{r}
such that

q\not\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{F}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}}q^{\prime}
for all
q^{\prime}\in G

then

21:

return

\text{NFP}(\mathcal{R})
is false
—
third
NFP
-condition violated

22:

if
there is
p_{1}^{\prime}\circ p_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{TS}
with

p_{1}^{\prime}\circ p_{2}^{\prime}\to p_{r}\in\mathcal{C}
and

p_{1}^{\prime}\circ p_{2}^{\prime}\neq p_{1}\circ p_{2}

then

23:

return

\text{NFP}(\mathcal{R})
is false
—
fourth
NFP
-condition violated

24:

return

{\color[rgb]{0.4688,0.4688,0.4688}\text{NFP}}(\mathcal{R})
is true

List of listings 14
Deciding
\text{NFP}(\mathcal{R})
.

Gray
parts differ from the
UNC
procedure (Listing
8
).

Example
\thethm

(continued from Examples
4
and
6.2
)
.

The underlying
UNC
procedure executes in the same way as Example
4
,
but with additional checks whenever an item is added to the
worklist
.
For
\mathcal{U}
,
after
([\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}},[\mathsf{b}],\mathsf{b})
is added to the
worklist
,
we find that
([\mathsf{b}])_{\mathcal{U}}=[\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}}
but not
[\mathsf{b}]\to[\mathsf{a}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{U}}
,
so the first
NFP
-condition is violated;
indeed we have
\mathsf{a}\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}\mathsf{b}
but not
\mathsf{a}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}\mathsf{b}
.
We would also have a violation of the second
NFP
-condition,
because
[\mathsf{f}]_{\mathcal{U}}\circ[\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}}\to[\mathsf{a}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{U}}
and

\mathsf{TS}([\mathsf{f}]_{\mathcal{U}}\circ[\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}})
.
A possible counterexample arising from this
is
\mathsf{f}\circ\mathsf{b}\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}\mathsf{b}
, but not
\mathsf{f}\circ\mathsf{b}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}\mathsf{b}
.

For
\mathcal{V}
, only
([\mathsf{f}]_{\mathcal{V}},[\mathsf{f}],\mathsf{f})
is added to the
worklist
,
and the corresponding checks of the first and second
NFP
-conditions
succeed.

Theorem
\thethm
.

The procedure in Listing
14

decides
\text{NFP}(\mathcal{R})
in
\mathcal{O}(\|\mathcal{R}\|^{3})
time.

Proof.

Listing
14
is an extension of Listing
8
.
In particular note that if the procedure returns that
\text{NFP}(\mathcal{R})

is true, then line
24
is reached,
so
UNC
holds for the input TRS as well;
in other words, whenever
\text{UNC}(\mathcal{R})
is false,
the procedure returns false as well.
So let us assume that
\text{UNC}(\mathcal{R})
is true and we have
a corresponding partial function
w:\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}\to\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\circ})

mapping convertibility classes to normal forms.

Compared to Listing
8
,
Listing
14
has additional checks on lines
6
–
9

and
19
–
23
.
The enumeration on line
5
covers
all constants
c
and states
q\in\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}
with
w(q)=c
,
noting that
c\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}}}[c]_{\mathcal{R}}
,
so
q=[c]_{\mathcal{R}}
is forced.
On lines
6
and
7
,
we check the first
NFP
-condition, where
s^{\prime}=p
,
noting that the only
\mathcal{E}^{-}
step leading to
c
is
[c]\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}}c
.
On lines
8
and
9
,
the second
NFP
-condition is checked.
By the proof of Theorem
4
(correctness of the
UNC
procedure),
Line
14
is executed exactly once for each state
q_{r}

for which
w(q_{r})
is defined but not a constant:
w(q_{r})=s_{1}\circ s_{2}
.
Lines
19
–
21
check the third
NFP
-condition
(the code uses
p_{1}\circ p_{2}\to p_{r}\in\mathcal{C}
instead of

q_{1}\circ q_{2}\to q_{r}\in\mathcal{C}
),
and lines
22
and
23
check the fourth
NFP
-condition
(using
p^{\prime}_{1}\circ p^{\prime}_{2}\to p_{r}\in\mathcal{C}
instead of

p_{1}\circ p_{2}\to q\in\mathcal{C}
).

For analyzing the complexity let
n=\|\mathcal{R}\|
.
Note that the additional precomputation takes cubic time,
and that the added checks in Listing
14
are executed
\mathcal{O}(n)
times
(see the proof of Theorem
4
).
The most expensive addition is the check of the third
NFP
-condition,
which may take
\mathcal{O}(n^{2})
time each time it is executed,
for a total of
\mathcal{O}(n^{3})
.
Overall the computation time is
\mathcal{O}(n^{3})
as claimed.
∎

Remark
\thethm
.

As far as we know, this is the first polynomial time algorithm for
deciding
NFP
for ground systems.

Remark
\thethm
.

Rather than making a copy of the
UNC
procedure with the modifications
in Listing
14
,
one can parameterize the
UNC
procedure with callbacks that are invoked
at lines
4
and
13
,
to avoid duplication of code.

7.
Deciding Confluence

We are given a finite ground TRS
\mathcal{R}
over a finite signature
\Sigma
.
As preprocessing, we curry and flatten the TRS in order to bound its arity
(Sections
3.1
and
3.2
),
obtaining
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
,
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\flat}
.
We then compute the congruence closure
\mathcal{C}
and rewrite closure
\mathcal{F}
,
enabling effective convertibility and reachability checking
(Sections
3.4
and
3.5
).
First we observe the following.

Lemma
\thethm
.

The TRS
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
is confluent
if and only if
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
is confluent.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
is confluent.
By Lemma
3.5
,

\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}
and
\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}
coincide,
so we show confluence (in fact, the Church-Rosser property)
of
\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
.
Assume that
s\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}]{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}t
.
This implies
s{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\circ}]{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}t{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}

by Proposition
3.2
.
Confluence of
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
implies that there is a term
u
with

s{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\circ}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}u\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\circ}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 20.48618pt\hskip-20.48618pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}}^{*}$}\hskip-20.48618pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 17.50014pt\hskip-17.50014pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}}^{*}$}\hskip-17.50014pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.33354pt\hskip-16.33354pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}}^{*}$}\hskip-16.33354pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}
.
Using Proposition
3.2
again
we obtain a joining sequence for
s
and
t

using rules from
\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
:

s\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}^{*}}s{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}u\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 36.47238pt\hskip-36.47238pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}}^{*}$}\hskip-36.47238pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 31.94478pt\hskip-31.94478pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}}^{*}$}\hskip-31.94478pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 30.77818pt\hskip-30.77818pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}}^{*}$}\hskip-30.77818pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 18.73618pt\hskip-18.73618pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}}}^{*}$}\hskip-18.73618pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 15.83345pt\hskip-15.83345pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}}}^{*}$}\hskip-15.83345pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 14.66685pt\hskip-14.66685pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}}}^{*}$}\hskip-14.66685pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t

Next assume that
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
is confluent.
By Lemma
3.5
, this implies confluence of
\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
.
Assume that
s\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\circ}]{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}t
,
where we may assume that
s,t\in\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\circ})
,
because confluence is preserved by signature extension.
Then
s\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}]{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}t
by Proposition
3.2
.
Consequently,
s\downarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}t
follows
by confluence of
\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
.
Using Proposition
3.2
again,

s=s{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\circ}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{*}}{\cdot}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{R}^{\circ}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 20.48618pt\hskip-20.48618pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}}^{*}$}\hskip-20.48618pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 17.50014pt\hskip-17.50014pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}}^{*}$}\hskip-17.50014pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.33354pt\hskip-16.33354pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}}^{*}$}\hskip-16.33354pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}}=t

7.1.
Conditions for Confluence

In this subsection, we derive necessary conditions for
confluence of
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
(and hence
\mathcal{R}
),
and then show that they are sufficient as well.

We follow the approach in
[
9
]
and
[
19
]
, which
is based on the analysis of two convertible terms
s
,
t
and their
normal forms with respect to a system of so-called
forward rules

of the rewrite closure, in our case using the system
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F}
.
Let us assume that
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
is confluent.
By Lemma
7
,
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
is confluent as well.
Let
s
and
t
be
\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}
-convertible terms and
let
s^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}
be
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F}
-normal forms of
s
and
t
.
Clearly,
s^{\prime}\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow[\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}]{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}t^{\prime}
follows.
Equivalently, there is a term
u
such that

(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}u\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}

(5)

Let us assume that
u\in\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}
.
To capture the conditions on
s^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}
,
we make use of the concept of top-stabilizable sides (Definition
6.1
).
If the sequence
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}u
is empty,
then
s^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}
;
otherwise, there are
s_{1},s_{2}\in\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}
with
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}s_{1}\circ s_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}u
,
which means that
s_{1}\circ s_{2}
is a top-stabilizable side.
Conversely,
for any
s_{1}\circ s_{2}\to u\in\mathcal{C}

where
s_{1}\circ s_{2}
is top-stabilizable,
we obtain a
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F}
-normal form
s^{\prime}
with
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}u

for which
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}u
holds.
An analogous analysis applies to
t^{\prime}
.
Because
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
is confluent,

s^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}
are joinable,
which by Lemma
3.5
implies

s^{\prime}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}^{*}}{\cdot}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 29.28482pt\hskip-29.28482pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}}^{*}$}\hskip-29.28482pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 24.8822pt\hskip-24.8822pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}}^{*}$}\hskip-24.8822pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 23.7156pt\hskip-23.7156pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}}^{*}$}\hskip-23.7156pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t^{\prime}

(6)

Furthermore, by Lemma
3.4
and Proposition
3.5
,
(
6
) implies

(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\cdot}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}

(7)

We distinguish three cases.

(1)

There are top-stabilizable sides

s_{1}\circ s_{2}
and
t_{1}\circ t_{2}

with corresponding
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F}
-normal forms
s^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}

such that

(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}s_{1}\circ s_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}u\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t_{1}\circ t_{2}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}

In this case, for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
the terms
s_{i}
and
t_{i}
are meetable by
\mathcal{C}
steps by (
7
),
which implies
s_{i}=t_{i}
noting that
\mathcal{C}
is confluent
and that
s_{i}
and
t_{i}
are
\mathcal{C}
-normal forms.

(2)

t^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}

and there is a top-stabilizable side
s_{1}\circ s_{2}

with associated
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F}
-normal form
s^{\prime}

such that

(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}s_{1}\circ s_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}u=(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}

To satisfy (
6
) and (
7
), there must be
t_{1},t_{2}\in\Sigma^{[]}
such that

t^{\prime}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}t_{1}\circ t_{2}
, and

(s_{i})_{\mathcal{R}}=(t_{i})_{\mathcal{R}}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.

(3)

s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}
with
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}=(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}
.
Then
s^{\prime}\downarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}t^{\prime}
.

Hence we obtain the following necessary conditions for
confluence of
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
:

Definition
\thethm
.

The
confluence conditions
for confluence of
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
are
as follows.

(1)

If
s_{1}\circ s_{2}
and
t_{1}\circ t_{2}
are top-stabilizable sides such that

s_{1}\circ s_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\cdot}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t_{1}\circ t_{2}

then
s_{i}=t_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.

(2)

If
s_{1}\circ s_{2}
is a top-stabilizable side
and
s_{1}\circ s_{2}\to(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\in\mathcal{C}

for
t^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}
,
then there must be
t_{1},t_{2}\in\Sigma^{[]}

such that
t^{\prime}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}t_{1}\circ t_{2}
, and

s_{i}=(t_{i})_{\mathcal{R}}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.

(3)

If
s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}
with
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}=(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}
then

s^{\prime}\downarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}t^{\prime}
.

Lemma
\thethm
.

The confluence conditions are necessary and sufficient for confluence
of
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
.

Proof.

Necessity has already been shown above.
For sufficiency, assume that the confluence conditions are satisfied.
We show confluence of
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}
,
from which confluence of
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}
follows by Lemma
7
.
Assume that there are terms
s,t
that are convertible
(
s\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow[\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}]{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}{\leftrightarrow_{\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\pm}}^{*}}t
) but not joinable.
Then any corresponding
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F}
-normal forms are not joinable either.
Let
s^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}
be convertible
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F}
-normal forms with no
common reduct such that
|s^{\prime}|+|t^{\prime}|
is minimal.
By Lemma
3.4
, we have

(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\cdot}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}

(8)

First note that
s^{\prime}\in\Sigma
(or
t^{\prime}\in\Sigma
) is impossible because
of the rules
c\to[c]\in\mathcal{E}
for
c\in\Sigma
.
We distinguish three cases.

(1)

If
s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}
.
Then
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}=(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}
from (
8
)
and the fact that
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}},(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\in\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}
are
\mathcal{C}
-normal forms.
Hence we obtain a joining sequence from the third confluence
condition, contradicting the non-joinability of
s^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
s^{\prime}=s_{1}^{\prime}\circ s_{2}^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}
, then
(
8
) becomes

(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}s_{1}\circ s_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}
,
noting that
(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}
is an
\mathcal{C}
-normal form.
In particular,
s_{1}\circ s_{2}
is a top-stabilizable side.
By the second confluence condition we obtain
a term
t_{1}\circ t_{2}
such that
t^{\prime}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}t_{1}\circ t_{2}
,
and
s_{i}=(t_{i})_{\mathcal{R}}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Because
(s_{1}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}s_{1}=(t_{1})_{\mathcal{R}}
,

t_{1}
and
s_{1}^{\prime}
are convertible by Lemma
3.4
.
Furthermore, since

|t_{1}|+|s_{1}^{\prime}|<|t^{\prime}|+|s^{\prime}|
, this implies that
t_{1}
and
s_{1}^{\prime}

are joinable. Analogously,
t_{2}
and
s_{2}^{\prime}
are also joinable, and
therefore
s^{\prime}
is joinable with
t_{1}\circ t_{2}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}]{}}{\mathrel{\hskip 27.88205pt\hskip-27.88205pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}$}\hskip-27.88205pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 23.56274pt\hskip-23.56274pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}$}\hskip-23.56274pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 22.39615pt\hskip-22.39615pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}}$}\hskip-22.39615pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}^{-}/\mathcal{F}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t^{\prime}
,
contradicting our assumptions.

(2
′
)

The case that
s^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}
and
t^{\prime}=t_{1}^{\prime}\circ t_{2}^{\prime}

is handled symmetrically.

(3)

If
s^{\prime}=s_{1}^{\prime}\circ s_{2}^{\prime}
and

t^{\prime}=t_{1}^{\prime}\circ t_{2}^{\prime}
, then (
8
) becomes

(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}r\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}
for some
r
.
If
r\in\Sigma
then
s^{\prime}=r=t^{\prime}
is trivially joinable.
If
r=r_{1}\circ r_{2}
(i.e.,
r
is not a constant), then
s_{1}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}^{\prime}

are convertible via
r_{1}
and
likewise
s_{2}^{\prime}
and
t_{2}^{\prime}
are convertible via
r_{2}
.
However, one of these pairs cannot be joinable, and we obtain a smaller
counterexample to confluence, a contradiction.
Therefore, we must have
r\in\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}
.
So (
8
) can be decomposed as

(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}s_{1}\circ s_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}r\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t_{1}\circ t_{2}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 16.20837pt\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-16.20837pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 13.26398pt\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-13.26398pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.09738pt\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}}}^{*}$}\hskip-12.09738pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}

In particular,
s_{1}\circ s_{2}
and
t_{1}\circ t_{2}
are top-stabilizable sides.
From the first confluence condition, we conclude that
s_{1}=t_{1}
and
therefore
s_{1}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}^{\prime}
are convertible.
By minimality of
|s^{\prime}|+|t^{\prime}|
,
s_{1}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}^{\prime}
must be joinable.
Analogously,
s_{2}^{\prime}
and
t_{2}^{\prime}
must also be joinable,
from which we conclude that
s^{\prime}=s_{1}^{\prime}\circ s_{2}^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}=t_{1}^{\prime}\circ t_{2}^{\prime}

are joinable as well, a contradiction.
∎

7.2.
Computation of Confluence Conditions

First we compute all top-stabilizable sides of shape
u\circ v
.
We have already done this in the decision procedure for
NFP
,
see Section
6.2
.
For the third confluence condition (cf. Definition
7.1
),
we have to identify constants
p,q\in\Sigma^{[]}
with

p\downarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}q
.

Note that
p\downarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}q
is equivalent to

p\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 29.28482pt\hskip-29.28482pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}}^{*}$}\hskip-29.28482pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 24.8822pt\hskip-24.8822pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}}^{*}$}\hskip-24.8822pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 23.7156pt\hskip-23.7156pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}}^{*}$}\hskip-23.7156pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\cdot}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}^{-}}^{*}}q
.
This matches the definition of meetable constants (Definition
5.1
),
with
\mathcal{F}
replaced by
\mathcal{F}^{-}
.
Consequently,
we can use the inference rules from Section
5.2

with
p\to q\in\mathcal{F}
replaced by
q\to p\in\mathcal{F}

for computing the joinable constants, see Figure
15
.

Definition
\thethm
.

We call
p,q\in\Sigma^{[]}

joinable constants

(written
p\downarrow q
)
if
p\downarrow_{\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}}q
.

\displaystyle p\downarrow p\mathstrut p\in\Sigma^{[]}\qquad p\downarrow q\lx@proof@logical@and p_{1}\circ p_{2}\to p\in\mathcal{E}p_{1}\downarrow q_{1}p_{2}\downarrow q_{2}q_{1}\circ q_{2}\to q\in\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle p\downarrow r\lx@proof@logical@and p\to q\in\mathcal{F}q\downarrow rr\in\Sigma^{[]}\qquad p\downarrow r\lx@proof@logical@and p\in\Sigma^{[]}p\downarrow qr\to q\in\mathcal{F}

Figure 15.
Inference rules for joinable constants

With the precomputation done,
checking the confluence conditions is straightforward.
Note that the map from
(\cdot)_{\mathcal{R}}:\Sigma^{[]}\to\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}

that is needed for the second and third confluence conditions
is a byproduct of the congruence closure computation.

Example
\thethm

(continued from Example
3.5
)
.

The joinability relations for
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
are given below.
As in Example
3.5
, the letters and superscripts indicate the
rule being used to derive the entry and computation stage.

{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{U}}=\begin{array}[]{r|ccccccc}&\mathsf{f}&\mathsf{a}&\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}&\mathsf{b}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{f}&r^{0}&&&\\
\mathsf{a}&&r^{0}&s^{1}_{r}&\\
\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}&&s^{1}_{l}&r^{0}&s^{1}_{l}\\
\mathsf{b}&&&s^{1}_{r}&r^{0}\\
\end{array}\qquad{\downarrow}_{\mathcal{V}}=\begin{array}[]{r|cccccccc}&\mathsf{f}&\mathsf{a}&\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}&\mathsf{b}&\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}&\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}&\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{f}&r^{0}&&&&&&\\
\mathsf{a}&&r^{0}&t_{l}^{1}&t_{l}^{1}&t_{l}^{1}&t_{l}^{1}&t_{l}^{1}\\
\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}&&t_{r}^{1}&r^{0}&t_{l}^{1}&c^{2}&c^{2}&c^{2}\\
\mathsf{b}&&t_{r}^{1}&t_{r}^{1}&r^{0}&t_{r}^{1}&c^{3}&c^{3}\\
\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}&&t_{r}^{1}&c^{2}&t_{l}^{1}&r^{0}&c^{2}&c^{4}\\
\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}&&t_{r}^{1}&c^{2}&c^{3}&c^{2}&r^{0}&c^{3}\\
\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}f{\mkern-1.5mu}b}&&t_{r}^{1}&c^{2}&c^{3}&c^{4}&c^{3}&r^{0}\\
\end{array}

It is now easy to verify that
\mathcal{U}
violates the third confluence condition
(we have
[\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}}=[\mathsf{b}]_{\mathcal{U}}
but not
[\mathsf{a}]\downarrow_{\mathcal{U}}[\mathsf{b}]
),
and therefore non-confluent.
The other two confluence conditions are satisfied for
\mathcal{U}
.
For the first confluence condition, note that
[\mathsf{f}]_{\mathcal{U}}\circ[\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}}

is the only top-stabilizable side.
The second confluence condition follows from
the fact that
s_{1}=[\mathsf{f}]_{\mathcal{U}}
,
s_{2}=[\mathsf{a}]_{\mathcal{U}}
,

t^{\prime}\in\{[\mathsf{a}],[\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}],[\mathsf{b}]\}
, and

[\mathsf{f}]\circ[\mathsf{a}]\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{U}}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{U}}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{U}}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{U}}}}[\mathsf{f{\mkern-1.5mu}a}]\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{U}}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{U}}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{U}}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{U}}}}[\mathsf{a}],[\mathsf{b}]
.
On the other hand,

\mathcal{V}
satisfies all confluence conditions and is, therefore, confluent.

Putting everything together, we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem
\thethm
.

Confluence of a ground TRS
\mathcal{R}
can be decided in cubic time.

Proof.

Let
n=\|\mathcal{R}\|
. We follow the process outlined above.
First we curry
\mathcal{R}
in linear time, obtaining
\mathcal{R}
with

\|\mathcal{R}\|\in\mathcal{O}(n)
.
Then we flatten
\mathcal{R}
, obtaining

(\mathcal{R}^{\flat},\mathcal{E})
with
\|\mathcal{E}\|\in\mathcal{O}(n)
and

\|\mathcal{R}^{\flat}\|\in\mathcal{O}(n)
in time
\mathcal{O}(n\log n)
.
In the next step we compute the rewrite and congruence closures

(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E})
of
(\mathcal{R}^{\flat},\mathcal{E})
in
\mathcal{O}(n^{3})
time.
We then compute
the joinable constants relation
\downarrow

(which are analogous to meetable constants),
and the top-stabilizable sides
\mathsf{TS}({-}\circ{-})
,
which as seen in Sections
5.2
and
6.2
takes
\mathcal{O}(n^{3})
time.
Finally we check the three confluence conditions.

(1)

For the first condition,
we check each of the
\mathcal{O}(n^{2})
pairs of rules

s_{1}\circ s_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}p,t_{1}\circ t_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}]{}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}}}q
with
p=q
,

\mathsf{TS}(s_{1}\circ s_{2})
and
\mathsf{TS}(t_{1}\circ t_{2})

and check that
s_{1}=t_{1}
and
s_{2}=t_{2}
.

(2)

For the second condition, we consider the
\mathcal{O}(n^{2})
triples

s_{1}\circ s_{2}\to p\in\mathcal{C}
,

t^{\prime}
with
p=(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}
,
and check that one of the
\mathcal{O}(n)
rules

t_{1}\circ t_{2}\to q\in\mathcal{E}
with
q\to t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}

satisfies
s_{1}=(t_{1})_{\mathcal{R}}
and
s_{2}=(t_{2})_{\mathcal{R}}
.

(3)

Finally, for the third condition,
for all
\mathcal{O}(n^{2})
pairs
s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{[]}

with
(s^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}=(t^{\prime})_{\mathcal{R}}

we check that
s^{\prime}\downarrow t^{\prime}
holds.

Overall these steps take
\mathcal{O}(n^{3})
time.
Correctness follows from Lemma
7.1
.
∎

Remark
\thethm
.

In our previous work
[
6
]
,
the congruence closure
\mathcal{C}
was computed as a rewrite closure,
resulting in a congruence relation
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}

such that for all convertible terms
s
and
t
,

s\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}^{*}}{\cdot}\mathchoice{\xleftarrow[\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}]{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 29.67717pt\hskip-29.67717pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}}^{*}$}\hskip-29.67717pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 25.19121pt\hskip-25.19121pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}}^{*}$}\hskip-25.19121pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 24.02461pt\hskip-24.02461pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\vphantom{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}}^{*}$}\hskip-24.02461pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{E}}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}t
.
In the present article, we instead work on the quotient obtained
by replacing each constant
[s]
by
[s]/\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=[s]_{\mathcal{R}}
.
This results in some simplification,
because
\mathcal{C}
is now deterministic,
but also in some complication,
because we are now working with two sets of extra constants,

\Sigma^{[]}
and
\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}}
,
which have to be related using the map
(\cdot)_{\mathcal{R}}
.
Apart from these differences,
the procedure presented here is identical to that presented in
[
6
]
.

8.
Related Work

8.1.
First-order Theory of Rewriting

The first-order theory of rewriting
was shown to be decidable by Dauchet and Tison
[
4
]
.
We can express the four properties of interest as follows.

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}}

\displaystyle\subseteq{\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\xleftarrow{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 14.59724pt\hskip-14.59724pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{*}$}\hskip-14.59724pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.15283pt\hskip-12.15283pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{*}$}\hskip-12.15283pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 10.98624pt\hskip-10.98624pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{*}$}\hskip-10.98624pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}}

(
CR
)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}}\cap(\mathcal{T}\times\mathsf{NF})

\displaystyle\subseteq{\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}}

(
NFP
)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\xleftrightarrow{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}{\leftrightarrow^{*}}}\cap(\mathsf{NF}\times\mathsf{NF})

\displaystyle\subseteq{\equiv}

(
UNC
)

\displaystyle({\mathchoice{\xleftarrow{*}}{\mathrel{\hskip 14.59724pt\hskip-14.59724pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{*}$}\hskip-14.59724pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 12.15283pt\hskip-12.15283pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{*}$}\hskip-12.15283pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hskip 10.98624pt\hskip-10.98624pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{*}$}\hskip-10.98624pt\hbox{$\vphantom{\leftarrow}{}^{\vphantom{*}}$}{\leftarrow}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}{\rightarrow^{*}}})\cap(\mathsf{NF}\times\mathsf{NF})

\displaystyle\subseteq{\equiv}

(
UNR
)

Here,
\mathcal{T}
denotes the set of all ground terms,

\mathsf{NF}
represents the set of all ground normal forms
with respect to the given ground TRS,
and
\equiv
stands for the identity relation on all terms.
We may assume that the maximum arity of the input TRS is at most two,
by currying the system first if necessary.
Thus, when following the procedure by Dauchet and Tison
[
4
]
,
we can construct automata for the left-hand and right-hand sides
of the subset tests in polynomial time.
However, in order to perform the subset test,
the right-hand side needs to be represented by a deterministic automaton.
For
CR
and
NFP
this incurs an exponential cost,
but for
UNC
and
UNR
, the automaton is already deterministic.
Consequently, for the latter two properties,
a polynomial time decision procedure is obtained.

Running times of

and

on 98 ground Cops

Based on Dauchet and Tison’s work,
the tool
FORT

[
18
]
implements decision procedures
for the first-order theory of rewriting
of left-linear, right-ground TRSs.
The algorithms described in this article are implemented in the
automated confluence prover
CSI

[
14
]
.
In Figure
16
,
we compare the running times of
FORT
to that of
CSI
on the
98 ground TRSs in the Cops database
2
2

2

More information on Cops can be found at

http://coco.nue.riec.tohoku.ac.jp/problems/

of
co
nfluence
p
roblem
s
.
(As the labels in Figure
16
indicate,
we tested each of the for properties
UNR
,
UNC
,
NFP
, and
CR
,
for both tools,
CSI
and
FORT
.)
Most of the problems are easy for both tools,
but while
CSI
never takes more than 0.5s on any of the input problems,

FORT
sometimes takes longer,
and even exceeds a timeout of 60 seconds on two of the confluence problems.
Full results are available online
3
3

3

http://cl-informatik.uibk.ac.at/users/bf3/ground/statistic.php

.

8.3.
UNR

UNR
was shown to be decidable in polynomial time
by Verma
[
21
]
and Godoy and Jaquemard
[
7
]
.
As far as we know, these are the best previously published bounds.
The main focus of Verma’s work is an abstract framework
for
UNC
and
UNR
that is also applicable to right-ground systems,
while Godoy and Jacquemard focus on
UNR
for linear shallow TRSs.

8.4.
UNC

As far as we know,
the fastest previous algorithm for deciding
UNC
of ground TRSs
is by Verma, Rusinovich and Lugiez
[
22
]
,
and takes
\mathcal{O}(\|\mathcal{R}\|^{2}\log\|\mathcal{R}\|)
time.
It is worth noting that the algorithm by Verma et al. is also based on the idea of using
a product construction for the intersection of the congruence closure
and an automaton recognizing normal forms.
However, the latter automaton is constructed in full,
leading to an essentially quadratic complexity bound.
Our algorithm is also closely related to another algorithm by Verma
[
21
, Theorem 31]
,
but some care is needed to achieve an almost linear bound.

8.5.
Confluence

To derive a polynomial time decision procedure for confluence of ground TRSs,
Comon et al.
[
3
]
use an approach based on a transformation by
Plaisted
[
17
]
that flattens the TRS.
Then they test
deep joinability
of sides of rules.
The authors sketch an implementation with complexity
\mathcal{O}(n^{5})
, where

n
is the size of the given TRS.
Tiwari
[
19
]
and Godoy et al.
[
9
]
base their approach on
a rewrite closure that constructs tree transducers—the given flattened TRS
\mathcal{R}

is converted into two TRSs
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{B}
such that
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{B}^{-1}

are left-flat, right-constant,
\mathcal{F}
is terminating,
and
{\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{R}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}^{*}}}={\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{F}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\xrightarrow[\mathcal{B}]{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{B}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{B}}^{*}}{\rightarrow_{\mathcal{B}}^{*}}}
. They then
consider
top-stabilizable
terms to derive conditions for confluence.
Tiwari obtains a bound of
\mathcal{O}(n^{9})
(but a more careful implementation would
end up with
\mathcal{O}(n^{6})
), while Godoy et al. obtain a bound of
\mathcal{O}(n^{6})
.
The algorithm of
[
3
]
is limited to ground TRSs, but

[
19
]
extends the algorithm to certain shallow, linear systems,
and
[
8
]
treats shallow, linear systems in full generality.
4
4

4

The same claim can be found in
[
9
]
. However, rule splitting,
a key step in the proof of their Lemma 3.1, only works if left-hand side and
right-hand side variables are disjoint for every rule.

In these extensions, however, the exponent depends on the maximum arity
of the function symbols of the given TRS. In our work we combine ideas
from
[
3
,
9
,
19
]
in order to improve the complexity bound
to
\mathcal{O}(n^{3})
. The key ingredients are a Plaisted-style rewrite closure,
which results in TRSs
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{B}
of only quadratic size
(namely
\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\mathcal{F}
), and
top-stabilizability, which is cheaper to test than deep joinability.

9.
Conclusion

We have presented efficient polynomial time decision procedures
for deciding normal form properties and confluence of ground TRSs.
In particular, we showed how to decide

UNR
in
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\log n)
time,

UNC
in
\mathcal{O}(n\log n)
time, and

NFP
and
CR
in
\mathcal{O}(n^{3})
time,
where
n=\|\mathcal{R}\|
is the size of the given ground TRS.
As far as we know,
the bounds for the normal form properties are improvements on the literature;
most notably,
we obtain the first polynomial bound for deciding
NFP
of ground TRSs.
The main innovation is the interleaving of
an enumeration of a potentially infinite set of witnesses
with checking conditions for the respective properties,
which also serve to ensure termination.
This is a theme that can be found in the procedures of
all three normal form properties presented here,
cf. Listings
8
,
11
,
13
and
14
.

There is a common theme in how the criteria are derived as well:
Starting with a conversion (or peak) that has a root step
(without loss of generality),
one uses the rewrite closure or congruence closure to restrict
which intermediate terms may be constants.
This opens the door to doing a top-down analysis of the intermediate terms,
ultimately giving rise to a finite criterion for the investigates property.
With the exception of
UNC
, wich property translates directly to
the fact that a certain tree automaton accepts at most one term in any state,
the criteria arise from a careful case analysis
(taking inspiration from previous work on confluence of ground TRSs),
with little intuitive understanding.

As future work,
it may be interesting to investigate whether these ideas apply to
the larger class of left-linear,
right-ground systems as treated by Verma in
[
21
]
.
It would also be interesting to prove correctness of the procedures
in an interactive theorem prover.
